\#\mathsf{Bad}_{K_{1}}(x,2^{-1}J)\leq M_{0}-1\quad\text{for all $x\in K_{1}$}

and

\mathcal{H}^{1}(K_{1})\geq\tau_{1}\coloneqq c_{0}\alpha A^{-2}\tau^{2}.

(B.9)

More generally, after
1\leq j\leq M_{0}
applications of Proposition
B.2
we get a compact set
K_{j}\subset K_{j-1}\subset\dots\subset K_{0}
with

\#\mathsf{Bad}_{K_{j}}(x,2^{-j}J)\leq M_{0}-j\quad\text{for all $x\in K_{j}$}

and

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}^{1}(K_{j})\geq\tau_{j}

\displaystyle\coloneqq c_{0}2^{-j}\alpha A^{-2}\tau_{j-1}^{2}

\displaystyle=c_{0}2^{-j}\alpha A^{-2}(c_{0}2^{-j-1}\alpha A^{-2}\tau_{j-2}^{2})^{2}

\displaystyle=(c_{0}\alpha A^{-2})^{1+2+\dots+2^{j-1}}2^{-j-2(j-1)-\dots-2^{j-1}\cdot 1}\tau^{2^{j}}

\displaystyle\geq(c_{0}\alpha A^{-2})^{2^{j}}2^{-2^{j+1}}\tau^{2^{j}}=(c_{0}^{\prime}\alpha A^{-2}\tau)^{2^{j}},

where in the last inequality we used the fact that
\sum_{i=0}^{j-1}2^{i}(j-i)\leq 2^{j+1}
.

Note that the set
G\coloneqq K_{M_{0}}\subset F
satisfies

\#\mathsf{Bad}_{G}(x,2^{-M_{0}}J)=0\quad\text{for all $x\in G$},

which means that
G
is contained in a Lipschitz graph
\Gamma
with
\lip(\Gamma)\lesssim 2^{M_{0}}\alpha^{-1}
. We also have

\mathcal{H}^{1}(F\cap\Gamma)\geq\mathcal{H}^{1}(G)\geq(c_{0}^{\prime}\alpha A^{-2}\tau)^{2^{M_{0}}}.

∎

Remark B.4
.

If we followed the proof of
[
MO18
, Proposition 1.12]
more closely, we would obtain a Lipschitz graph
\Gamma
with
\lip(\Gamma)\sim\alpha^{-1}
. However, this would entail applying Proposition
B.2
roughly
2^{M_{0}}
times, instead of
M_{0}
times, and we would get another exponential loss in (
B.1
).

Appendix C
Intersections of lower content regular sets

We say that
E\subset\mathbb{R}^{2}
is lower content regular if
\mathcal{H}^{1}_{\infty}(E\cap B(x,r))\gtrsim r
for all
x\in E
,
0<r<\diam(E)
. It is easy to see that curves and Ahlfors regular sets are lower content regular.

Lemma C.1
.

Suppose that
E\subset\mathbb{R}^{2}
is lower content regular, and
\Gamma\subset\mathbb{R}^{2}
is arbitrary. Then, for
0<\delta<\diam(E)

\mathcal{H}^{1}_{\infty}(E\cap\Gamma(3\delta))\gtrsim\mathcal{H}^{1}_{\infty}(E(\delta)\cap\Gamma).

Remark C.2
.

In particular, if
E
is Ahlfors regular and
\Gamma
is a curve, then

\mathcal{H}^{1}_{\infty}(E\cap\Gamma(3\delta))\gtrsim\mathcal{H}^{1}_{\infty}(E(\delta)\cap\Gamma)\gtrsim\mathcal{H}^{1}_{\infty}(E\cap\Gamma(\delta/3)).

Proof.

Let
\gamma=E(\delta)\cap\Gamma
and
F=E\cap\gamma(3\delta)\subset E\cap\Gamma(3\delta).
We will show that
\mathcal{H}^{1}_{\infty}(F)\gtrsim\mathcal{H}^{1}_{\infty}(\gamma)
.

Without loss of generality assume
\mathcal{H}^{1}_{\infty}(F)<\infty
, otherwise there is nothing to prove. Let
\{B_{i}=B(x_{i},r_{i})\}_{i\in I}
be a covering of
F
consisting of balls such that
\sum_{i\in I}r_{i}\lesssim\mathcal{H}^{1}_{\infty}(F)
. By slightly increasing the radii, we may assume that
x_{i}\in F
for all
i\in I
. Our goal is to modify this family of balls to obtain a nice covering of
\gamma
.

The main observation is that we may tweak the covering
\{B_{i}\}_{i\in I}
a little to get a covering
\mathcal{B}^{\prime\prime}
such that
\sum_{B\in\mathcal{B}^{\prime\prime}}r(B)\lesssim\mathcal{H}^{1}_{\infty}(F)
and
r(B)\geq\delta
for all
B\in\mathcal{B}^{\prime\prime}
. To see that this is the case, let
I_{small}=\{i\in I\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}r_{i}<\delta\}
,
I_{big}=I\setminus I_{small}
, and

I_{bad}=\{i\in I_{small}\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}B(x_{i},10\delta)\cap B_{j}=\varnothing\quad\text{for all }j\in I_{big}\}.

It follows from the triangle inequality that

\bigcup_{i\in I_{small}\setminus I_{bad}}B_{i}\subset\bigcup_{i\in I_{big}}20B_{i}.

Consider

\mathcal{B}=\{B(x_{i},5\delta)\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}i\in I_{bad}\}\cup\{20B_{i}\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}i\in I_{big}\},

so that
\mathcal{B}
is still a cover of
F
.

By the 5
r
-covering lemma, we may find a family of disjoint balls
\mathcal{B}^{\prime}\subset\mathcal{B}
such that
\{5B\}_{B\in\mathcal{B}^{\prime}}
covers
F
. We claim that

\sum_{B\in\mathcal{B}^{\prime}}r(B)\lesssim\sum_{i\in I}r_{i}\lesssim\mathcal{H}^{1}_{\infty}(F).

Let
I_{0}\subset I_{big}
be such that
20B_{i}\in\mathcal{B}^{\prime}
for
i\in I_{0}
, and
I_{1}\subset I_{bad}
be such that
B(x_{i},5\delta)\in\mathcal{B}^{\prime}
for
i\in I_{1}
. It is clear that
\sum_{i\in I_{0}}r(20B_{i})\lesssim\sum_{i\in I}r_{i}
, so we only need to show that

\sum_{i\in I_{1}}\delta=\#I_{1}\cdot\delta\lesssim\sum_{i\in I}r_{i}.

(C.1)

Recalling that
F=E\cap\gamma(3\delta)
and
\gamma=E(\delta)\cap\Gamma
, and also that
x_{i}\in F
, we get that for every
i
there exists
y_{i}\in\gamma
such that
|x_{i}-y_{i}|\leq 3\delta
, and
z_{i}\in E
such that
|y_{i}-z_{i}|\leq\delta
. Then, for any
i\in I
we have

\mathcal{H}^{1}_{\infty}(B(x_{i},10\delta)\cap F)\geq\mathcal{H}^{1}_{\infty}(B(y_{i},5\delta)\cap F)=\mathcal{H}^{1}_{\infty}(B(y_{i},5\delta)\cap E\cap\gamma(3\delta))\\
\geq\mathcal{H}^{1}_{\infty}(B(y_{i},3\delta)\cap E)\geq\mathcal{H}^{1}_{\infty}(B(z_{i},2\delta)\cap E)\gtrsim\delta,

where in the last line we used the lower content regularity of
E
.
At the same time, since
\{B_{j}\}_{j\in I}
is a cover of
F
, for
i\in I_{bad}
we get

\delta\lesssim\mathcal{H}^{1}_{\infty}(B(x_{i},10\delta)\cap F)\leq\sum_{j\in I\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}B_{j}\cap B(x_{i},10\delta)\neq\varnothing}r_{j}=\sum_{j\in I_{small}\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}B_{j}\cap B(x_{i},10\delta)\neq\varnothing}r_{j},

where in the last equality we used the fact that
i\in I_{bad}
.

For every
i\in I_{1}\subset I_{bad}
set
J(i)=\{j\in I_{small}\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}B_{j}\cap B(x_{i},10\delta)\neq\varnothing\}
. Recalling that for
j\in I_{small}
we have
r(B_{j})=r_{j}<\delta
, it follows that if
j\in J(i)
, then
B_{j}\subset B(x_{i},20\delta)
. Since the balls
\{B(x_{i},5\delta)\}_{i\in I_{1}}
are disjoint, the balls
\{B(x_{i},20\delta)\}_{i\in I_{1}}
have bounded overlap. Thus, every
j\in I_{small}
belongs to at most a bounded number of families
J(i),i\in I_{1}
. This gives

\sum_{i\in I_{1}}\delta\lesssim\sum_{i\in I_{1}}\sum_{j\in J(i)}r_{j}\lesssim\sum_{j\in I_{small}}r_{j},

which is the desired estimate (
C.1
). Now,
\mathcal{B}^{\prime\prime}\coloneqq\{5B\}_{B\in\mathcal{B}^{\prime}}
is a cover of
F
satisfying
\sum_{B\in\mathcal{B}^{\prime\prime}}r(B)\lesssim\mathcal{H}^{1}_{\infty}(F)
and
r(B)\geq\delta
for all
B\in\mathcal{B}^{\prime\prime}
.

To finish the proof, observe that
\{2B\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}B\in\mathcal{B}^{\prime\prime}\}
is a covering of
\gamma=E(\delta)\cap\Gamma
. Indeed, for every
x\in\gamma
there exists
y\in E
with
|x-y|\leq\delta
. In particular,
y\in E\cap\gamma(\delta)\subset F
. Taking
B
such that
y\in B
we immediately get
x\in 2B
because
r(B)\geq\delta
. Hence, by the definition of Hausdorff content

\mathcal{H}^{1}_{\infty}(\gamma)\leq\sum_{B\in\mathcal{B}^{\prime\prime}}r(2B)\lesssim\mathcal{H}^{1}_{\infty}(F).

∎
